  --- Day 2 discussion ---

  Noah: I agree that Quinn's behavior is suspicious, but I also noticed Noah being unusually defensive when asked about the night before. We should consider both of them and look for more clues before making a hasty decision.
  Bob: I think we should look at patterns of behavior rather than just isolated moments. Quinn's silence could be strategic, but Noah's defensiveness also raises red flags. Let's ask others if they've noticed anything unusual about either of them. We need more evidence before we vote.
  Iris: I’ve been paying attention to who’s been trying to steer the conversation and who’s avoiding it. Quinn’s silence is notable, but so is Noah’s defensiveness. If we’re going to vote someone out, we need to be sure. Let’s check if anyone else has noticed strange behavior from either of them.
  Quinn: I’ve noticed that both Quinn and Noah are acting strangely, but I also saw Mia being overly confident early on, almost like she knew something. Maybe she’s hiding her own role? Let’s not jump to conclusions — we need to dig deeper and see who’s trying to control the narrative.
  Paul: I investigated Quinn last night and found them to be a Villager. While Quinn's silence is suspicious, there's no evidence they are a werewolf. Noah's defensiveness is also worth looking into, but we need more concrete clues before making a decision. Let's focus on gathering more information rather than rushing to vote.

=== Werewolf — Day 2 | DAY — Discussion ===

Living players (8): Mia, Noah, Bob, Iris, Quinn, Paul, Zoe, Jack

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Mia: I think we need to focus on who's been acting suspiciously. Someone is definitely hiding something. I don't trust Quinn — they've been too quiet and avoid eye contact. Let's vote them out.
  Noah: I agree that Quinn's behavior is suspicious, but I also noticed Noah being unusually defensive when asked about the night before. We should consider both of them and look for more clues before making a hasty decision.
  Bob: I think we should look at patterns of behavior rather than just isolated moments. Quinn's silence could be strategic, but Noah's defensiveness also raises red flags. Let's ask others if they've noticed anything unusual about either of them. We need more evidence before we vote.
  Iris: I’ve been paying attention to who’s been trying to steer the conversation and who’s avoiding it. Quinn’s silence is notable, but so is Noah’s defensiveness. If we’re going to vote someone out, we need to be sure. Let’s check if anyone else has noticed strange behavior from either of them.
  Quinn: I’ve noticed that both Quinn and Noah are acting strangely, but I also saw Mia being overly confident early on, almost like she knew something. Maybe she’s hiding her own role? Let’s not jump to conclusions — we need to dig deeper and see who’s trying to control the narrative.
  Paul: I investigated Quinn last night and found them to be a Villager. While Quinn's silence is suspicious, there's no evidence they are a werewolf. Noah's defensiveness is also worth looking into, but we need more concrete clues before making a decision. Let's focus on gathering more information rather than rushing to vote.
  Zoe: I think we need to be careful about jumping to conclusions based on behavior alone. While Quinn's silence and Noah's defensiveness are notable, Paul just confirmed Quinn is a Villager. That gives us a solid clue — let's focus on Noah now. His defensiveness could be a sign of guilt. We should push him for more information and see how he reacts.

It is your turn to speak.  Share your observations, suspicions, or arguments. Be concise.
Respond with: STATEMENT: <your message>

[COMPLETION]
STATEMENT: I appreciate the focus on Noah's defensiveness, but let's not forget that werewolves can also act defensive when cornered. Paul confirmed Quinn is a Villager, so we should be cautious about targeting someone based on behavior alone. I think we need to continue gathering information and look for more concrete evidence before making a final decision.

[ACTION={'statement': "I appreciate the focus on Noah's defensiveness, but let's not forget that werewolves can also act defensive when cornered. === Werewolf — Day 2 | DAY — Vote to eliminate ===

Living players (8): Mia, Noah, Bob, Iris, Quinn, Paul, Zoe, Jack

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Mia: I think we need to focus on who's been acting suspiciously. Someone is definitely hiding something. I don't trust Quinn — they've been too quiet and avoid eye contact. Let's vote them out.
  Noah: I agree that Quinn's behavior is suspicious, but I also noticed Noah being unusually defensive when asked about the night before. We should consider both of them and look for more clues before making a hasty decision.
  Bob: I think we should look at patterns of behavior rather than just isolated moments. Quinn's silence could be strategic, but Noah's defensiveness also raises red flags. Let's ask others if they've noticed anything unusual about either of them. We need more evidence before we vote.
  Iris: I’ve been paying attention to who’s been trying to steer the conversation and who’s avoiding it. Quinn’s silence is notable, but so is Noah’s defensiveness. If we’re going to vote someone out, we need to be sure. Let’s check if anyone else has noticed strange behavior from either of them.
  Quinn: I’ve noticed that both Quinn and Noah are acting strangely, but I also saw Mia being overly confident early on, almost like she knew something. Maybe she’s hiding her own role? Let’s not jump to conclusions — we need to dig deeper and see who’s trying to control the narrative.
  Paul: I investigated Quinn last night and found them to be a Villager. While Quinn's silence is suspicious, there's no evidence they are a werewolf. Noah's defensiveness is also worth looking into, but we need more concrete clues before making a decision. Let's focus on gathering more information rather than rushing to vote.
  Zoe: I think we need to be careful about jumping to conclusions based on behavior alone. While Quinn's silence and Noah's defensiveness are notable, Paul just confirmed Quinn is a Villager. That gives us a solid clue — let's focus on Noah now. His defensiveness could be a sign of guilt. We should push him for more information and see how he reacts.
  Jack: I appreciate the focus on Noah's defensiveness, but let's not forget that werewolves can also act defensive when cornered. Paul confirmed Quinn is a Villager, so we should be cautious about targeting someone based on behavior alone. I think we need to continue gathering information and look for more concrete evidence before making a final decision.

Your investigation results:
  Quinn: Villager

  Noah: I agree that Quinn's behavior is suspicious, but I also noticed Noah being unusually defensive when asked about the night before. We should consider both of them and look for more clues before making a hasty decision.
  Bob: I think we should look at patterns of behavior rather than just isolated moments. Quinn's silence could be strategic, but Noah's defensiveness also raises red flags. Let's ask others if they've noticed anything unusual about either of them. We need more evidence before we vote.
  Iris: I’ve been paying attention to who’s been trying to steer the conversation and who’s avoiding it. Quinn’s silence is notable, but so is Noah’s defensiveness. If we’re going to vote someone out, we need to be sure. Let’s check if anyone else has noticed strange behavior from either of them.
  Quinn: I’ve noticed that both Quinn and Noah are acting strangely, but I also saw Mia being overly confident early on, almost like she knew something. Maybe she’s hiding her own role? Let’s not jump to conclusions — we need to dig deeper and see who’s trying to control the narrative.
  Paul: I investigated Quinn last night and found them to be a Villager. While Quinn's silence is suspicious, there's no evidence they are a werewolf. Noah's defensiveness is also worth looking into, but we need more concrete clues before making a decision. Let's focus on gathering more information rather than rushing to vote.
  Zoe: I think we need to be careful about jumping to conclusions based on behavior alone. While Quinn's silence and Noah's defensiveness are notable, Paul just confirmed Quinn is a Villager. That gives us a solid clue — let's focus on Noah now. His defensiveness could be a sign of guilt. We should push him for more information and see how he reacts.
  Jack: I appreciate the focus on Noah's defensiveness, but let's not forget that werewolves can also act defensive when cornered. Paul confirmed Quinn is a Villager, so we should be cautious about targeting someone based on behavior alone. I think we need to continue gathering information and look for more concrete evidence before making a final decision.
  --- Day 3 discussion ---
  Mia: I think we’re focusing too much on behavior and not enough on the facts. Paul confirmed Quinn was a Villager, so we can’t ignore that. Noah’s defensiveness is suspicious, but we need more than that. Let’s not let fear drive us — we should look for someone with clear evidence, not just shadows of doubt.
  Noah: Paul confirmed Quinn was a Villager, so we can't let suspicion alone guide our next move. However, Noah's defensiveness and the lack of clear evidence pointing to anyone else make him the most logical target. If we don't act now, the werewolves might manipulate the narrative again. Let's vote out Noah.
  Bob: I agree that we need to focus on facts, not just suspicion. Paul confirmed Quinn was a Villager, so we can't let fear or defensiveness alone dictate our next move. However, Noah's behavior has raised red flags, and if we don't address it now, the werewolves could continue to manipulate the group. Let's vote out Noah and see if that clears up any confusion.
  Iris: I think we need to be cautious. While Noah's defensiveness is concerning, we still don't have concrete evidence against him. The werewolves could be trying to create division by making us target the wrong person. Let's keep looking for more clues before we act.
  Paul: Paul confirmed Quinn is a Villager, and my investigation also shows Noah is a Villager. While Noah’s defensiveness is notable, we have no evidence he is a werewolf. Let’s not let suspicion cloud our judgment. We need to look for someone with clearer signs — not just behavior alone.
  Zoe: Paul confirmed both Quinn and Noah are Villagers, so we need to look beyond behavior and focus on who still has the potential to be a werewolf. Let’s not let defensiveness or silence be our only clues. We need to investigate further and consider who might be hiding their role without obvious tells.
  Jack: I think we're overlooking the bigger picture. Paul confirmed Quinn and Noah are villagers, but that doesn't mean the werewolves aren't still among us. Mia and I are the only ones left who could be werewolves, and I’ve noticed her being overly confident early on — almost like she knew something. Let’s not forget that werewolves can manipulate the narrative, and Mia’s behavior is worth looking into. Let’s keep her in our sights.
  --- Day 4 discussion ---

Living players (4): Bob, Iris, Zoe, Jack

  Day 2: Quinn (was Villager) was eliminated by vote.
  Day 3: Mia (was Werewolf) was eliminated by vote.
  Day 3: Noah (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 4: Paul (was Seer) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Mia: I think we need to focus on who's been acting suspiciously. Someone is definitely hiding something. I don't trust Quinn — they've been too quiet and avoid eye contact. Let's vote them out.
  Noah: I agree that Quinn's behavior is suspicious, but I also noticed Noah being unusually defensive when asked about the night before. We should consider both of them and look for more clues before making a hasty decision.
  Bob: I think we should look at patterns of behavior rather than just isolated moments. Quinn's silence could be strategic, but Noah's defensiveness also raises red flags. Let's ask others if they've noticed anything unusual about either of them. We need more evidence before we vote.
  Iris: I’ve been paying attention to who’s been trying to steer the conversation and who’s avoiding it. Quinn’s silence is notable, but so is Noah’s defensiveness. If we’re going to vote someone out, we need to be sure. Let’s check if anyone else has noticed strange behavior from either of them.
  Quinn: I’ve noticed that both Quinn and Noah are acting strangely, but I also saw Mia being overly confident early on, almost like she knew something. Maybe she’s hiding her own role? Let’s not jump to conclusions — we need to dig deeper and see who’s trying to control the narrative.
  Paul: I investigated Quinn last night and found them to be a Villager. While Quinn's silence is suspicious, there's no evidence they are a werewolf. Noah's defensiveness is also worth looking into, but we need more concrete clues before making a decision. Let's focus on gathering more information rather than rushing to vote.
  Zoe: I think we need to be careful about jumping to conclusions based on behavior alone. While Quinn's silence and Noah's defensiveness are notable, Paul just confirmed Quinn is a Villager. That gives us a solid clue — let's focus on Noah now. His defensiveness could be a sign of guilt. We should push him for more information and see how he reacts.
  Jack: I appreciate the focus on Noah's defensiveness, but let's not forget that werewolves can also act defensive when cornered. Paul confirmed Quinn is a Villager, so we should be cautious about targeting someone based on behavior alone. I think we need to continue gathering information and look for more concrete evidence before making a final decision.
  --- Day 3 discussion ---
  Mia: I think we’re focusing too much on behavior and not enough on the facts. Paul confirmed Quinn was a Villager, so we can’t ignore that. Noah’s defensiveness is suspicious, but we need more than that. Let’s not let fear drive us — we should look for someone with clear evidence, not just shadows of doubt.
  Noah: Paul confirmed Quinn was a Villager, so we can't let suspicion alone guide our next move. However, Noah's defensiveness and the lack of clear evidence pointing to anyone else make him the most logical target. If we don't act now, the werewolves might manipulate the narrative again. Let's vote out Noah.
  Bob: I agree that we need to focus on facts, not just suspicion. Paul confirmed Quinn was a Villager, so we can't let fear or defensiveness alone dictate our next move. However, Noah's behavior has raised red flags, and if we don't address it now, the werewolves could continue to manipulate the group. Let's vote out Noah and see if that clears up any confusion.
  Iris: I think we need to be cautious. While Noah's defensiveness is concerning, we still don't have concrete evidence against him. The werewolves could be trying to create division by making us target the wrong person. Let's keep looking for more clues before we act.
  Paul: Paul confirmed Quinn is a Villager, and my investigation also shows Noah is a Villager. While Noah’s defensiveness is notable, we have no evidence he is a werewolf. Let’s not let suspicion cloud our judgment. We need to look for someone with clearer signs — not just behavior alone.
  Zoe: Paul confirmed both Quinn and Noah are Villagers, so we need to look beyond behavior and focus on who still has the potential to be a werewolf. Let’s not let defensiveness or silence be our only clues. We need to investigate further and consider who might be hiding their role without obvious tells.
  Jack: I think we're overlooking the bigger picture. Paul confirmed Quinn and Noah are villagers, but that doesn't mean the werewolves aren't still among us. Mia and I are the only ones left who could be werewolves, and I’ve noticed her being overly confident early on — almost like she knew something. Let’s not forget that werewolves can manipulate the narrative, and Mia’s behavior is worth looking into. Let’s keep her in our sights.
  --- Day 4 discussion ---
  Bob: Paul has confirmed Quinn and Noah are villagers, so we can't let suspicion alone drive our next move. Mia's confidence early on and her push to vote out Quinn raise red flags. We need to focus on who still has the potential to be a werewolf — and Mia's behavior is worth investigating further. Let's not ignore the possibility that she might be hiding something.
  Iris: I’ve been paying close attention to the patterns of behavior and the flow of information. While Mia’s confidence early on is suspicious, we need to be careful not to let assumptions cloud our judgment. The werewolves are still among us, and we must rely on facts, not just hunches. Let’s continue to gather evidence and not rush into another vote based on speculation.
  Paul: Paul and I have confirmed Quinn and Noah are villagers, so we must focus on others. Mia's early confidence and push to vote out Quinn raise concerns. We need to investigate her further and consider her as a suspect. Let's not let assumptions guide us, but we also can't ignore patterns. Mia's behavior is worth looking into.
  Zoe: Mia's early confidence and her push to vote out Quinn seem suspicious, especially since she was eliminated by the group later. Werewolves often try to manipulate the narrative, and her behavior aligns with that. We need to focus on her and consider her as a potential threat. Let's not let fear or assumptions drive us, but Mia's actions deserve closer scrutiny.
  Jack: I’ve been observing the group dynamics closely, and while Mia’s confidence early on is notable, I think we need to be careful not to let past behavior dictate our current decisions. We’ve already confirmed Quinn and Noah are villagers, so we should focus on who remains and look for new patterns. Let’s not jump to conclusions — we need more solid evidence before we act.
  --- Day 5 discussion ---
  Bob: I think we need to focus on Mia now. Her early confidence and push to vote out Quinn were suspicious, and she was eliminated by the group later — which could mean she was trying to shift blame. With Quinn and Noah confirmed as villagers, Mia is the most likely remaining suspect. Let's investigate her behavior and consider her as the next target.

You are playing Werewolf (social deduction game).

Your role: Werewolf.  Side: WEREWOLF.

OBJECTIVE: Eliminate enough villagers so werewolves equal or outnumber them.
  - Each night: choose a player to kill.
  - During the day: avoid being voted out.

GAME FLOW: Night (werewolves kill, seer sees, doctor saves) → Day (discussion + vote). Repeat until one side wins.

[USER]
=== Werewolf — Day 6 | DAY — Discussion ===

Living players (3): Bob, Iris, Jack

  Day 2: Quinn (was Villager) was eliminated by vote.
  Day 3: Mia (was Werewolf) was eliminated by vote.
  Day 3: Noah (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 4: Paul (was Seer) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 5: Zoe (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Mia: I think we need to focus on who's been acting suspiciously. Someone is definitely hiding something. I don't trust Quinn — they've been too quiet and avoid eye contact. Let's vote them out.
  Noah: I agree that Quinn's behavior is suspicious, but I also noticed Noah being unusually defensive when asked about the night before. We should consider both of them and look for more clues before making a hasty decision.
  Bob: I think we should look at patterns of behavior rather than just isolated moments. Quinn's silence could be strategic, but Noah's defensiveness also raises red flags. Let's ask others if they've noticed anything unusual about either of them. We need more evidence before we vote.
  Iris: I’ve been paying attention to who’s been trying to steer the conversation and who’s avoiding it. Quinn’s silence is notable, but so is Noah’s defensiveness. If we’re going to vote someone out, we need to be sure. Let’s check if anyone else has noticed strange behavior from either of them.
  Quinn: I’ve noticed that both Quinn and Noah are acting strangely, but I also saw Mia being overly confident early on, almost like she knew something. Maybe she’s hiding her own role? Let’s not jump to conclusions — we need to dig deeper and see who’s trying to control the narrative.
  Paul: I investigated Quinn last night and found them to be a Villager. While Quinn's silence is suspicious, there's no evidence they are a werewolf. Noah's defensiveness is also worth looking into, but we need more concrete clues before making a decision. Let's focus on gathering more information rather than rushing to vote.
  Zoe: I think we need to be careful about jumping to conclusions based on behavior alone. While Quinn's silence and Noah's defensiveness are notable, Paul just confirmed Quinn is a Villager. That gives us a solid clue — let's focus on Noah now. His defensiveness could be a sign of guilt. We should push him for more information and see how he reacts.
  Jack: I appreciate the focus on Noah's defensiveness, but let's not forget that werewolves can also act defensive when cornered. Paul confirmed Quinn is a Villager, so we should be cautious about targeting someone based on behavior alone. I think we need to continue gathering information and look for more concrete evidence before making a final decision.
  --- Day 3 discussion ---
  Mia: I think we’re focusing too much on behavior and not enough on the facts. Paul confirmed Quinn was a Villager, so we can’t ignore that. Noah’s defensiveness is suspicious, but we need more than that. Let’s not let fear drive us — we should look for someone with clear evidence, not just shadows of doubt.
  Noah: Paul confirmed Quinn was a Villager, so we can't let suspicion alone guide our next move. However, Noah's defensiveness and the lack of clear evidence pointing to anyone else make him the most logical target. If we don't act now, the werewolves might manipulate the narrative again. Let's vote out Noah.
  Bob: I agree that we need to focus on facts, not just suspicion. Paul confirmed Quinn was a Villager, so we can't let fear or defensiveness alone dictate our next move. However, Noah's behavior has raised red flags, and if we don't address it now, the werewolves could continue to manipulate the group. Let's vote out Noah and see if that clears up any confusion.
  Iris: I think we need to be cautious. While Noah's defensiveness is concerning, we still don't have concrete evidence against him. The werewolves could be trying to create division by making us target the wrong person. Let's keep looking for more clues before we act.
  Paul: Paul confirmed Quinn is a Villager, and my investigation also shows Noah is a Villager. While Noah’s defensiveness is notable, we have no evidence he is a werewolf. Let’s not let suspicion cloud our judgment. We need to look for someone with clearer signs — not just behavior alone.
  Zoe: Paul confirmed both Quinn and Noah are Villagers, so we need to look beyond behavior and focus on who still has the potential to be a werewolf. Let’s not let defensiveness or silence be our only clues. We need to investigate further and consider who might be hiding their role without obvious tells.
  Jack: I think we're overlooking the bigger picture. Paul confirmed Quinn and Noah are villagers, but that doesn't mean the werewolves aren't still among us. Mia and I are the only ones left who could be werewolves, and I’ve noticed her being overly confident early on — almost like she knew something. Let’s not forget that werewolves can manipulate the narrative, and Mia’s behavior is worth looking into. Let’s keep her in our sights.
  --- Day 4 discussion ---
  Bob: Paul has confirmed Quinn and Noah are villagers, so we can't let suspicion alone drive our next move. Mia's confidence early on and her push to vote out Quinn raise red flags. We need to focus on who still has the potential to be a werewolf — and Mia's behavior is worth investigating further. Let's not ignore the possibility that she might be hiding something.
  Iris: I’ve been paying close attention to the patterns of behavior and the flow of information. While Mia’s confidence early on is suspicious, we need to be careful not to let assumptions cloud our judgment. The werewolves are still among us, and we must rely on facts, not just hunches. Let’s continue to gather evidence and not rush into another vote based on speculation.
  Paul: Paul and I have confirmed Quinn and Noah are villagers, so we must focus on others. Mia's early confidence and push to vote out Quinn raise concerns. We need to investigate her further and consider her as a suspect. Let's not let assumptions guide us, but we also can't ignore patterns. Mia's behavior is worth looking into.
  Zoe: Mia's early confidence and her push to vote out Quinn seem suspicious, especially since she was eliminated by the group later. Werewolves often try to manipulate the narrative, and her behavior aligns with that. We need to focus on her and consider her as a potential threat. Let's not let fear or assumptions drive us, but Mia's actions deserve closer scrutiny.
  Jack: I’ve been observing the group dynamics closely, and while Mia’s confidence early on is notable, I think we need to be careful not to let past behavior dictate our current decisions. We’ve already confirmed Quinn and Noah are villagers, so we should focus on who remains and look for new patterns. Let’s not jump to conclusions — we need more solid evidence before we act.
  --- Day 5 discussion ---
  Bob: I think we need to focus on Mia now. Her early confidence and push to vote out Quinn were suspicious, and she was eliminated by the group later — which could mean she was trying to shift blame. With Quinn and Noah confirmed as villagers, Mia is the most likely remaining suspect. Let's investigate her behavior and consider her as the next target.
  Iris: I’ve been tracking the patterns of behavior and the flow of information. Mia’s early confidence and her push to vote out Quinn were suspicious, especially since she was later eliminated by the group — which could mean she was trying to shift blame. With Quinn and Noah confirmed as villagers, Mia is the most likely remaining suspect. Let’s focus on her and consider her as the next target.
  Zoe: Mia's early confidence and push to vote out Quinn were suspicious, and her elimination by the group could mean she was trying to shift blame. With Quinn and Noah confirmed as villagers, Mia is the most likely remaining suspect. Let's focus on her and consider her as the next target.
  Jack: I think we're focusing too much on Mia. While her confidence was notable, we have no concrete evidence she's a werewolf. The real threat could still be among us — maybe even someone who's been acting normal the whole time. Let's not make another mistake based on suspicion alone.
  --- Day 6 discussion ---
  Bob: I think we need to focus on Mia. Her early confidence and push to vote out Quinn were suspicious, and she was eliminated by the group later — which could mean she was trying to shift blame. With Quinn and Noah confirmed as villagers, Mia is the most likely remaining suspect. Let's vote her out.
  Iris: I've observed that Mia's early confidence and push to vote out Quinn were suspicious, especially since she was later eliminated by the group — which could mean she was trying to shift blame. With Quinn and Noah confirmed as villagers, Mia is the most likely remaining suspect. Let's vote her out.

It is your turn to speak.  Share your observations, suspicions, or arguments. Be concise.
Respond with: STATEMENT: <your message>
